### Kotlin Suspending Listener Example

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/site/listener/index.html

Demonstrates a Kotlin listener with suspend functions for handling player join and quit events, interacting with a database for data operations. It showcases how to integrate suspend functions within Bukkit event listeners.

```kotlin
import org.bukkit.event.EventHandler
import org.bukkit.event.Listener
import org.bukkit.event.player.PlayerJoinEvent
import org.bukkit.event.player.PlayerQuitEvent
import java.util.*

class PlayerDataListener(private val database: Database) : Listener {
    @EventHandler
    suspend fun onPlayerJoinEvent(event: PlayerJoinEvent) {
        val player = event.player
        val playerData = database.getDataFromPlayer(player)
        playerData.name = player.name
        playerData.lastJoinDate = Date()
        database.saveData(player, playerData)
    }

    @EventHandler
    suspend fun onPlayerQuitEvent(event: PlayerQuitEvent) {
        val player = event.player
        val playerData = database.getDataFromPlayer(player)
        playerData.name = player.name
        playerData.lastQuitDate = Date()
        database.saveData(player, playerData)
    }
}
```

### Manual Shutdown Strategy for Long-Running Jobs

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/site/plugindisable/index.html

Illustrates how to set the `shutdownStrategy` to `MANUAL` to prevent the plugin from shutting down coroutines automatically. This allows for waiting on long-running jobs, such as the `longRunningJob` in this example, to complete before the plugin is disabled.

```kotlin
private var longRunningJob: Job? = null

override fun onEnable() {
    val plugin = this
    plugin.mcCoroutineConfiguration.shutdownStrategy = ShutdownStrategy.MANUAL

    longRunningJob = plugin.launch {
        delay(10000)
        println("Over")
    }
}

override fun onDisable() {
    runBlocking {
        longRunningJob!!.join()
    }

    val plugin = this
    plugin.mcCoroutineConfiguration.disposePluginSession()
}
```

### Coroutine Launching

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/apidocs/mccoroutine-root/com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.bukkit/index.html

Launches a new coroutine on the Minecraft main thread.

```APIDOC
launch:
  launch(context: CoroutineContext = minecraftDispatcher, start: Any = CoroutineStart.DEFAULT, block: suspend Any.() -> Unit): Any
    Description: Launches a new coroutine on the minecraft main thread without blocking the current thread and returns a reference to the coroutine as a Job. The coroutine is cancelled when the resulting job is Job.cancel.
    Parameters:
      context: The CoroutineContext to use for the coroutine (defaults to minecraftDispatcher).
      start: The CoroutineStart strategy (defaults to CoroutineStart.DEFAULT).
      block: The suspend function block to execute as the coroutine.
    Returns: A Job reference to the launched coroutine.
```

### Full MiniGame Class Implementation

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/docs/tasks.md

This is a complete implementation of the MiniGame class in Kotlin. It includes methods for joining players, starting the game, running the game loop, stopping the game, and sending messages to players. It utilizes coroutines for asynchronous operations like delays.

```kotlin
import kotlinx.coroutines.delay
import org.bukkit.entity.Player

class MiniGame {
    private var isStarted = false;
    private var players = HashSet<Player>()
    private var remainingTime = 0

    fun join(player: Player) {
        if (isStarted) {
            return
        }

        players.add(player)
    }

    suspend fun start() {
        if (isStarted) {
            return
        }

        isStarted = true

        // This loop represents a traditional repeating task which ticks every 1 second and is called 20 times.
        for (i in 0 until 20) {
            sendMessageToPlayers("Game is starting in ${20 - i} seconds.")
            delay(1000)
        }

        // ... Teleport players to game.
        run()
    }

    private suspend fun run() {
        remainingTime = 300 // 300 seconds

        while (isStarted && remainingTime > 0) {
            sendMessageToPlayers("Game is over in ${remainingTime} seconds.")
            delay(1000)
            remainingTime--
        }

        if (!isStarted) {
            sendMessageToPlayers("Game was cancelled by external stop.")
        }

        isStarted = false
        // ... Teleport players back to lobby.
    }

    fun stop() {
        if (!isStarted) {
            return
        }

        isStarted = false
    }

    private fun sendMessageToPlayers(message: String) {
        players.forEach { p -> p.sendMessage(message) }
    }
}
```

### MiniGameListener for Player Join Events

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/docs/tasks.md

This Kotlin class implements the Listener interface to handle Bukkit events. Specifically, it listens for PlayerJoinEvent and calls the 'join' and 'start' methods of the MiniGame class. It uses suspending functions for event handling.

```kotlin
import org.bukkit.event.EventHandler
import org.bukkit.event.Listener
import org.bukkit.event.player.PlayerJoinEvent

class MiniGameListener(private val miniGame: MiniGame) : Listener {
    @EventHandler
    suspend fun onPlayerJoinEvent(playerJoinEvent: PlayerJoinEvent) {
        miniGame.join(playerJoinEvent.player)

        // Just for testing purposes
        miniGame.start()
    }
}
```

### MiniGame Class Implementation

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/site/tasks/index.html

The `MiniGame` class manages the game state, including player joining, starting the game with a countdown, running the game loop, and stopping the game. It utilizes Kotlin coroutines (`suspend` functions and `delay`) for asynchronous operations.

```kotlin
import kotlinx.coroutines.delay
import org.bukkit.entity.Player

class MiniGame {
    private var isStarted = false
    private var players = HashSet<Player>()
    private var remainingTime = 0

    fun join(player: Player) {
        if (isStarted) {
            return
        }
        players.add(player)
    }

    suspend fun start() {
        if (isStarted) {
            return
        }
        isStarted = true
        for (i in 0 until 20) {
            sendMessageToPlayers("Game is starting in ${20 - i} seconds.")
            delay(1000)
        }
        run()
    }

    private suspend fun run() {
        remainingTime = 300
        while (isStarted && remainingTime > 0) {
            sendMessageToPlayers("Game is over in ${remainingTime} seconds.")
            delay(1000)
            remainingTime--
        }
        if (!isStarted) {
            sendMessageToPlayers("Game was cancelled by external stop.")
        }
        isStarted = false
    }

    fun stop() {
        if (!isStarted) {
            return
        }
        isStarted = false
    }

    private fun sendMessageToPlayers(message: String) {
        players.forEach { p -> p.sendMessage(message) }
    }
}
```


### Fabric Server Mod Initialization

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/docs/plugin.md

Shows how to initialize MCCoroutine within a Fabric server mod, integrating with the Minecraft scheduler and handling server lifecycle events.

```kotlin
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.fabric.launch
import net.fabricmc.api.DedicatedServerModInitializer
import net.minecraft.server.MinecraftServer
import net.fabricmc.fabric.api.event.lifecycle.v1.ServerLifecycleEvents
import java.util.concurrent.Executor

class MCCoroutineSampleServerMod : DedicatedServerModInitializer {
    override fun onInitializeServer() {
        // Connect Native Minecraft Scheduler and MCCoroutine.
        mcCoroutineConfiguration.minecraftExecutor = Executor { r ->
            server.submitAndJoin(r)
        }
        launch {
            onServerStarting(server)
        }

        ServerLifecycleEvents.SERVER_STOPPING.register { server ->
            mcCoroutineConfiguration.disposePluginSession()
        }
    }
    /**
     * MCCoroutine is ready after the server has started.
     */
    private suspend fun onServerStarting(server : MinecraftServer) {
        // Minecraft Main Thread
        val database = Database()
        database.createDbIfNotExist()
    }
}
```

### Starting Coroutine on Folia with Entity Dispatcher

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/site/coroutine/index.html

Launches a coroutine using the entity's specific dispatcher in Folia, ensuring execution on the correct thread for that entity. Falls back to the Bukkit main thread dispatcher on standard Bukkit servers. This approach manages plugin-specific data access and entity interactions safely in a multithreaded environment.

```kotlin
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.folia.launch
import org.bukkit.plugin.Plugin
import kotlinx.coroutines.withContext

fun foo(entity : Entity) {
    plugin.launch { // or plugin.launch(plugin.mainDispatcher) {}
        // Your plugin main thread. If you have already been on your plugin main thread, this scope is entered immidiatly.
        // Regardless if your are on bukkit or on folia, this is your personal thread and you must not call bukkit methods on it.
        // Now perform some data access on your plugin data like accessing a repository.
        val storedEntityDataInDatabase = database.get()

        // Apply the data on the entity thread using the entityDispatcher.
        // The plugin.entityDispatcher(entity) parameter ensures, that we end up on the scheduler for the entity in the specific region.
        withContext(plugin.entityDispatcher(entity)) {
              // In Folia, this will be the correct thread for the given entity.
              // In Bukkit, this will be the main thread.
        }
    }
}
```

--------------------------------

### Minestom Suspending Listener Example

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/site/listener/index.html

Demonstrates how to use the `addSuspendingListener` extension method in Minestom to register listeners that can utilize suspend functions. This allows for asynchronous operations within event handlers, improving responsiveness. Note that timing measurements might be affected for suspendable functions.

```kotlin
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.minestom.addSuspendingListener
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.minestom.launch
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.minestom.sample.extension.impl.Database
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.minestom.sample.extension.impl.PlayerDataListener
import net.minestom.server.MinecraftServer
import net.minestom.server.event.player.PlayerLoginEvent

fun main(args: Array<String>) {
    val minecraftServer = MinecraftServer.init()

    minecraftServer.launch {
        val database = Database()
        // Minecraft Main Thread
        database.createDbIfNotExist()

        val listener = PlayerDataListener(database)
        MinecraftServer.getGlobalEventHandler()
            .addSuspendingListener(minecraftServer, PlayerLoginEvent::class.java) { e ->
                listener.onPlayerJoinEvent(e)
            }
    }

    minecraftServer.start("0.0.0.0", 25565)
}
```

### Switch MCCoroutine to Test Driver

Demonstrates how to switch MCCoroutine to its test implementation by setting the `MCCoroutine.Driver` to `TestMCCoroutine.Driver`. This change affects all tests within the current session and is essential for running tests without a live server environment.

```kotlin
import org.junit.jupiter.api.Test

class MyExampleTest {

     init {
        /**
         * This switches MCCoroutine to the test implementation of MCCoroutine.
         * It affects all the tests in the current session.
         */
        MCCoroutine.Driver = TestMCCoroutine.Driver
    }

    @Test
    fun testCase01(){
    }
}
```

### Theme Toggling and Initialization

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/apidocs/mccoroutine-root/com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.bungeecord/-m-c-coroutine-exception-event/exception.html

Handles theme toggling based on local storage and OS preference, and initializes the page by replacing 'no-js' with 'js' class.

```javascript
var pathToRoot = "../../../\n";
document.documentElement.classList.replace("no-js","js");
const storage = localStorage.getItem("dokka-dark-mode")
if (storage == null) {
    const osDarkSchemePreferred = window.matchMedia && window.matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)').matches
    if (osDarkSchemePreferred === true) {
        document.getElementsByTagName("html")[0].classList.add("theme-dark")
    }
} else {
    const savedDarkMode = JSON.parse(storage)
    if(savedDarkMode === true) {
        document.getElementsByTagName("html")[0].classList.add("theme-dark")
    }
}
```

### Bukkit Coroutine Launch Execution Order

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/docs/coroutine.md

Illustrates the execution flow and order of operations when using `plugin.launch` in Bukkit, including the use of `delay` and suspend functions.

```kotlin
// This is a Bukkit example, but it works in the same way in every other framework.
class Foo(private val plugin: Plugin) {

    fun bar() {
        // Main Thread
        // If you have been on the minecraft main thread before calling plugin.launch, this scope is entered immediately without any delay.
        println("I am first")

        val job = plugin.launch {
            println("I am second") // The context is not suspended when switching to the same suspendable context.
            delay(1000)
            println("I am fourth") // The context is given back after 1000 milliseconds and continuous here.
            bob()
        }

        // When calling delay the suspendable context is suspended and the original context immediately continuous here.
        println("I am third")
    }

    private suspend fun bob() {
        println("I am fifth")
    }
}
```

```kotlin
"I am first"
"I am second"
"I am third"
"I am fourth"
"I am fifth"
```

### Bukkit Dispatchers and Context Switching

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/docs/coroutine.md

Demonstrates using MCCoroutine's `minecraftDispatcher` and `Dispatchers.IO` for context switching within Bukkit coroutines. Shows how to launch coroutines on the main thread and perform asynchronous operations.

```kotlin
fun foo() {
    plugin.launch {
        // This will always be on the minecraft main thread.
        // If you have been on the minecraft main thread before calling plugin.launch, this scope is entered immediately without any delay.

        val result1 = withContext(plugin.minecraftDispatcher) {
            // Perform operations on the minecraft main thread.
            "Player is " // Optionally, return a result.
        }

        // Here we are automatically back on the main thread again.

        // Prefer using Dispatchers.IO instead of asyncDispatcher 
        val result2 = withContext(Dispatchers.IO) {
            // Perform operations asynchronously.
            " Max"
        }

        // Here we are automatically back on the main thread again.

        println(result1 + result2) // Prints 'Player is Max'
    }
}
```

```kotlin
@EventHandler
fun onPlayerJoinEvent(event: PlayerJoinEvent) {
    plugin.launch {
        // This will always be on the minecraft main thread.
        // A PlayerJoinEvent arrives on the main thread, therefore this scope is entered immediately without any delay.

        val name = event.player.name
        val listOfFriends = withContext(Dispatchers.IO) {
            // IO Thread
            val friendNames = Files.readAllLines(Paths.get("$name.txt"))
            friendNames
        }

        // Main Thread
        val friendText = listOfFriends.joinToString(", ")
        event.player.sendMessage("My friends are: $friendText")
    }
}
```

### CoroutineTimings API Documentation

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/apidocs/mccoroutine-root/com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.bukkit/-coroutine-timings/index.html

Provides documentation for the CoroutineTimings class, its constructors, properties, and methods. This class is crucial for integrating Kotlin Coroutines with Spigot's timing system.

```APIDOC
CoroutineTimings
abstract class CoroutineTimings : AbstractCoroutineContextElement, Runnable

The spigot timings require a reference to the runnable to display the name of timings correctly. Now, Kotlin Coroutines does not allow to directly pass a runnable object, because a single coroutine may consist out of multiple runnables. This class is a workaround coroutine context element, which can be passed along the minecraftDispatcher to display a valid name for the coroutine.

Constructors:
  CoroutineTimings()
    Link copied to clipboard
    constructor()

Types:
  Key
  object Key : CoroutineContext.Key<CoroutineTimings>
    Link copied to clipboard
    Key identifier of the context element.

Properties:
  key
    open override val key: CoroutineContext.Key<*>
      Link copied to clipboard
      Key identifier of the context element.
  queue
    var queue: Queue<Runnable>
      Link copied to clipboard
      Multiple tasks can be assigned to a single coroutine. We implement this by a queue.

Functions:
  fold
    open override fun <R> fold(initial: R, operation: (R, CoroutineContext.Element) -> R): R
      Link copied to clipboard

  get
    open operator override fun <E : CoroutineContext.Element> get(key: CoroutineContext.Key<E>): E?
      Link copied toදා clipboard

  minusKey
    open override fun minusKey(key: CoroutineContext.Key<*>): CoroutineContext
      Link copied to clipboard

  plus
    open operator fun plus(context: CoroutineContext): CoroutineContext
      Link copied to clipboard

  run
    open override fun run()
      Link copied to clipboard
      When an object implementing interface Runnable is used to create a thread, starting the thread causes the object's run method to be called in that separately executing thread.
```

### MCCoroutine Initialization and Theme Handling

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/site/coroutine/index.html

This snippet demonstrates MCCoroutine's JavaScript-based theme handling and initialization logic, including retrieving and applying color palettes from local storage.

```javascript
var palette = __md_get("__palette");
if (palette && palette.color) {
    if ("(prefers-color-scheme)" === palette.color.media) {
        var media = matchMedia("(prefers-color-scheme: light)");
        var input = document.querySelector(media.matches ? "[data-md-color-media='(prefers-color-scheme: light)']" : "[data-md-color-media='(prefers-color-scheme: dark)']");
        palette.color.media = input.getAttribute("data-md-color-media");
        palette.color.scheme = input.getAttribute("data-md-color-scheme");
        palette.color.primary = input.getAttribute("data-md-color-primary");
        palette.color.accent = input.getAttribute("data-md-color-accent");
    }
    for (var [key, value] of Object.entries(palette.color)) {
        document.body.setAttribute("data-md-color-" + key, value);
    }
}
```

### Velocity Plugin Initialization

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/docs/plugin.md

Demonstrates initializing a Velocity plugin with MCCoroutine, manually initializing the coroutine scope and handling proxy initialization events.

```kotlin
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.velocity.SuspendingPluginContainer
import com.velocitypowered.api.event.proxy.ProxyInitializeEvent
import com.velocitypowered.api.event.Subscribe
import com.velocitypowered.api.plugin.Plugin
import javax.inject.Inject

@Plugin(
    id = "mccoroutinesample",
    name = "MCCoroutineSample",
    description = "MCCoroutineSample is sample plugin to use MCCoroutine in Velocity."
)
class MCCoroutineSamplePlugin {
    private val database = Database()

     @Inject
    constructor(suspendingPluginContainer: SuspendingPluginContainer) {
        suspendingPluginContainer.initialize(this)
    }

    @Subscribe
    suspend fun onProxyInitialization(event: ProxyInitializeEvent) {
        // Velocity Thread Pool
        database.createDbIfNotExist()
    }
}
```

```javascript
var pathToRoot = "../../";
document.documentElement.classList.replace("no-js","js");
const storage = localStorage.getItem("dokka-dark-mode");
if (storage == null) {
    const osDarkSchemePreferred = window.matchMedia && window.matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)').matches;
    if (osDarkSchemePreferred === true) {
        document.getElementsByTagName("html")[0].classList.add("theme-dark");
    }
} else {
    const savedDarkMode = JSON.parse(storage);
    if(savedDarkMode === true) {
        document.getElementsByTagName("html")[0].classList.add("theme-dark");
    }
}
```

### Minestom Server Launch with MCCoroutine

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/docs/plugin.md

Shows how to launch a coroutine scope using MCCoroutine's `launch` extension function on the Minestom MinecraftServer instance. This enables suspendable operations within the server's context.

```kotlin
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.minestom.launch
import net.minestom.server.MinecraftServer

fun main(args: Array<String>) {
    val minecraftServer = MinecraftServer.init()
    minecraftServer.launch {
        // Suspendable operations   
    }
    minecraftServer.start("0.0.0.0", 25565)
}
```

```APIDOC
  CoroutineSession:

  MCCoroutine:
    Hidden internal MCCoroutine interface.

  MCCoroutineConfiguration:
    Additional configurations for MCCoroutine and communication.

  MCCoroutineExceptionEvent:
    A Fabric event which is called when an exception is raised in one of the coroutines managed by MCCoroutine. Cancelling this exception causes the error to not get logged and offers to possibility for custom logging.

  SuspendingCommand<S>:
    A suspending interface of the default command.

Enums:

    See https://shynixn.github.io/MCCoroutine/wiki/site/plugindisable for more details.

Properties:
  mcCoroutineConfiguration:
    Gets the configuration instance of MCCoroutine.
    Type: MCCoroutineConfiguration

  minecraftDispatcher:
    Gets the extension minecraft dispatcher.
    Type: CoroutineContext

  scope:
    Gets the coroutine scope.
    Type: unknown class

Extension Properties:
  Int.ticks:
    Type: Long
```

### Bukkit Plugin Launch Execution Order

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/site/coroutine/index.html

Demonstrates the execution flow of `plugin.launch` on the main thread in a Bukkit environment. It highlights how coroutines are launched, suspended, and resumed, including the use of `delay` and switching between contexts.

```kotlin
class Foo(private val plugin: Plugin) {

    fun bar() {
        // Main Thread
        // If you have been on the minecraft main thread before calling plugin.launch, this scope is entered immediately without any delay.
        println("I am first")

        val job = plugin.launch {
            println("I am second") // The context is not suspended when switching to the same suspendable context.
            delay(1000)
            println("I am fourth") // The context is given back after 1000 milliseconds and continuous here.
            bob()
        }

        // When calling delay the suspendable context is suspended and the original context immediately continuous here.
        println("I am third")
    }

    private suspend fun bob() {
        println("I am fifth")
    }
}
```

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/apidocs/mccoroutine-root/com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.bukkit/index.html

Provides documentation for the Bukkit-specific interfaces and classes within the MCCoroutine library. This includes CoroutineSession, CoroutineTimings, EventExecutionType, MCCoroutine, MCCoroutineConfiguration, MCCoroutineExceptionEvent, ShutdownStrategy, SuspendingCommandExecutor, SuspendingJavaPlugin, SuspendingPlugin, and SuspendingTabCompleter.

```APIDOC
Package: com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.bukkit

Types:
  interface CoroutineSession
    Facade of a coroutine session of a single plugin.

  abstract class CoroutineTimings : AbstractCoroutineContextElement, Runnable
    The spigot timings require a reference to the runnable to display the name of timings correctly. This class is a workaround coroutine context element, which can be passed along the minecraftDispatcher to display a valid name for the coroutine.

  enum EventExecutionType : Enum<EventExecutionType>
    The mode how suspendable events are dispatched manually.

  interface MCCoroutine
    Hidden internal MCCoroutine interface.

  interface MCCoroutineConfiguration
    Additional configurations for MCCoroutine and communication.

  class MCCoroutineExceptionEvent(val plugin: Any, val exception: Throwable)
    A Bukkit event which is called when an exception is raised in one of the coroutines managed by MCCoroutine. Cancelling this exception causes the error to not get logged and offers the possibility for custom logging.

  enum ShutdownStrategy : Enum<ShutdownStrategy>
    See https://shynixn.github.io/MCCoroutine/wiki/site/plugindisable for more details.

  interface SuspendingCommandExecutor
    Represents a class which contains a single method for executing commands.

  open class SuspendingJavaPlugin : SuspendingPlugin
    Extension to the JavaPlugin for suspendable lifecycle functions.

  interface SuspendingPlugin
    Extension to the plugin interface for suspendable lifecycle functions.

  interface SuspendingTabCompleter
    Represents a suspending class which can suggest tab completions for commands.

Properties:
  val asyncDispatcher: CoroutineContext
    Gets the plugin async dispatcher.

  val mcCoroutineConfiguration: MCCoroutineConfiguration
    
```

### Database Operations (Bukkit)

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/docs/plugin.md

Implements asynchronous database operations for Bukkit plugins. It includes methods to create the database if it doesn't exist, retrieve player data, and save player data, ensuring IO operations are performed on a separate thread.

```kotlin
import kotlinx.coroutines.Dispatchers
import kotlinx.coroutines.withContext
import org.bukkit.entity.Player
import java.util.*

class Database() {
    suspend fun createDbIfNotExist() {
        println("[createDbIfNotExist] Start on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
        withContext(Dispatchers.IO){
            println("[createDbIfNotExist] Creating database on database io thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
            // ... create tables
        }
        println("[createDbIfNotExist] End on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
    }

    suspend fun getDataFromPlayer(player : Player) : PlayerData {
        println("[getDataFromPlayer] Start on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
        val playerData = withContext(Dispatchers.IO) {
            println("[getDataFromPlayer] Retrieving player data on database io thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
            // ... get from database by player uuid or create new playerData instance.
            PlayerData(player.uniqueId, player.name, Date(), Date())
        }

        println("[getDataFromPlayer] End on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
        return playerData;
    }
  
    suspend fun saveData(player : Player, playerData : PlayerData) {
        println("[saveData] Start on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)

        withContext(Dispatchers.IO){
            println("[saveData] Saving player data on database io thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
            // insert or update playerData
        }

        println("[saveData] End on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
    }
}
```

### Initialize Dark Mode and Dispatcher

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/apidocs/mccoroutine-root/com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.bungeecord/bungee-cord-dispatcher.html

Handles the initialization of dark mode based on local storage or OS preference, and retrieves the bungeeCord dispatcher for coroutine context.

```javascript
var pathToRoot = "../../";
document.documentElement.classList.replace("no-js","js");
const storage = localStorage.getItem("dokka-dark-mode");
if (storage == null) {
    const osDarkSchemePreferred = window.matchMedia && window.matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)').matches;
    if (osDarkSchemePreferred === true) {
        document.getElementsByTagName("html")[0].classList.add("theme-dark");
    }
} else {
    const savedDarkMode = JSON.parse(storage);
    if(savedDarkMode === true) {
        document.getElementsByTagName("html")[0].classList.add("theme-dark");
    }
}
```

### Using MCCoroutine in Tests with Dispatchers

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/docs/unittests.md

Demonstrates how to use MCCoroutine's coroutine dispatchers within a test environment, including `runBlocking`, `withContext`, and `launch`. It mocks a `Plugin` instance and utilizes `minecraftDispatcher` and `asyncDispatcher`.

```kotlin
import org.junit.jupiter.api.Test
import org.mockito.Mockito
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.core.MCCoroutine
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.core.TestMCCoroutine
import kotlinx.coroutines.runBlocking
import kotlinx.coroutines.withContext
import kotlinx.coroutines.delay
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.core.launch
import kotlinx.coroutines.Dispatchers
import org.bukkit.plugin.Plugin

class MyExampleTest {
    
    init {
        /**
         * This switches MCCoroutine to the test implementation of MCCoroutine.
         * It affects all the tests in the current session.
         */
        MCCoroutine.Driver = TestMCCoroutine.Driver
    }
    
    @Test
    fun testCase01(){
        // Uses the mocking library called Mockito to mock a plugin instance.
        // It does not matter how you create a plugin instance. Other mocking libraries work as well.
        val plugin = Mockito.mock(Plugin::class.java)

        // We need to use runBlocking here, otherwise the test exits early
        runBlocking(plugin.minecraftDispatcher) {
            println("Step 1: " + Thread.currentThread().name + "/" + Thread.currentThread().id)

            withContext(Dispatchers.IO) {
                println("Step 2: " + Thread.currentThread().name + "/" + Thread.currentThread().id)
                delay(1000)
            }

            println("Step 3: " + Thread.currentThread().name + "/" + Thread.currentThread().id)

            // As always, prefer using Dispatchers.IO instead of plugin.asyncDispatcher.
            withContext(plugin.asyncDispatcher) {
                println("Step 4: " + Thread.currentThread().name + "/" + Thread.currentThread().id)
                delay(1000)
            }

            println("Step 5: " + Thread.currentThread().name + "/" + Thread.currentThread().id)

            // Just as an example, we can also use plugin.launch
            plugin.launch {
                println("Step 6: " + Thread.currentThread().name + "/" + Thread.currentThread().id)
                delay(1000)
                println("Step 7: " + Thread.currentThread().name + "/" + Thread.currentThread().id)
            }.join() // Wait until finished.
        }
    }
}
```

### Suspending Event Handling

```APIDOC
callSuspendingEvent:
  callSuspendingEvent(event: Any, plugin: Any): Collection<Any>
    Description: Calls an event with the given details. If there are multiple suspend event receivers, each receiver is executed concurrently. Allows to await the completion of suspending event listeners.
    Parameters:
      event: The event to call.
      plugin: The plugin associated with the event.
    Returns: A collection of results from the event listeners.

  callSuspendingEvent(event: Any, plugin: Any, eventExecutionType: EventExecutionType): Collection<Any>

    Parameters:

      plugin: The plugin associated with the event.
      eventExecutionType: The type of execution for the event.
    Returns: A collection of results from the event listeners.

      plugin: The plugin associated with the listener.
```

### SuspendingCommandExecutor.onCommand

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/apidocs/mccoroutine-root/com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.bukkit/-suspending-command-executor/on-command.html

Executes the given command, returning its success. If false is returned, then the "usage" plugin.yml entry for this command (if defined) will be sent to the player.

```APIDOC
onCommand
abstract suspend fun [onCommand](on-command.html)(sender: <Error class: unknown class>, command: <Error class: unknown class>, label: [String](https://kotlinlang.org/api/latest/jvm/stdlib/kotlin-stdlib/kotlin/-string/index.html), args: [Array](https://kotlinlang.org/api/latest/jvm/stdlib/kotlin-stdlib/kotlin/-array/index.html)<out [String](https://kotlinlang.org/api/latest/jvm/stdlib/kotlin-stdlib/kotlin/-string/index.html)>): [Boolean](https://kotlinlang.org/api/latest/jvm/stdlib/kotlin-stdlib/kotlin/-boolean/index.html)

Executes the given command, returning its success. If false is returned, then the "usage" plugin.yml entry for this command (if defined) will be sent to the player.

#### Return

True if a valid command, otherwise false.

#### Parameters

sender
*   Source of the command.
    
command
*   Command which was executed.
    
label
*   Alias of the command which was used.
    
args
*   Passed command arguments.
    
```

### Bukkit Coroutine Launch

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/docs/coroutine.md

Launches a coroutine on the Bukkit platform. This coroutine will execute on the main Minecraft thread.

```kotlin
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.bukkit.launch
import org.bukkit.plugin.java.JavaPlugin

fun foo(plugin: JavaPlugin) {
    plugin.launch {
        // In Bukkit, this will be the main thread.
    }
}
```

### SuspendingPlugin API Documentation

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/apidocs/mccoroutine-root/com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.bukkit/-suspending-plugin/index.html

Defines the interface for plugins that support suspendable lifecycle functions. It includes abstract methods for asynchronous plugin initialization, enabling, and disabling.

```APIDOC
interface SuspendingPlugin
  Extension to the plugin interface for suspendable lifecycle functions.

  Inheritors:
    SuspendingJavaPlugin

  Functions:
    onDisableAsync()
      abstract suspend fun onDisableAsync()
      Called when this plugin is disabled.

    onEnableAsync()
      abstract suspend fun onEnableAsync()
      Called when this plugin is enabled

    onLoadAsync()
      abstract suspend fun onLoadAsync()
      Called after a plugin is loaded but before it has been enabled.
```

### Minestom Command Launch

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/docs/commandexecutor.md

Demonstrates creating a traditional command in Minestom and using `server.launch` or `extension.launch` within the addSyntax handler for asynchronous operations.

```kotlin
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.minestom.launch
import net.minestom.server.MinecraftServer
import net.minestom.server.command.builder.Command
import net.minestom.server.command.builder.arguments.ArgumentType
import net.minestom.server.entity.Player

// Example usage within a command registration:
val command = Command("mycommand")
command.addSyntax({
    // Use server.launch or extension.launch here for async operations
    MinecraftServer.getServer().scheduler.launch {
        // Your asynchronous code here
    }
}, ArgumentType.Literal("mysubcommand"))
```

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/apidocs/mccoroutine-root/com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.velocity/-m-c-coroutine/disable-logging.html

This JavaScript code initializes the page by replacing 'no-js' with 'js' in the document class. It also handles dark mode preferences based on local storage or operating system settings.

```javascript
var pathToRoot = "../../../";
document.documentElement.classList.replace("no-js","js");
const storage = localStorage.getItem("dokka-dark-mode")
if (storage == null) {
    const osDarkSchemePreferred = window.matchMedia && window.matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)').matches
    if (osDarkSchemePreferred === true) {
        document.getElementsByTagName("html")[0].classList.add("theme-dark")
    }
} else {
    const savedDarkMode = JSON.parse(storage)
    if(savedDarkMode === true) {
        document.getElementsByTagName("html")[0].classList.add("theme-dark")
    }
}
```

### MCCoroutine Initialization and Theme Handling

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/site/caching/index.html

Initializes MCCoroutine by setting up theme preferences from local storage and handling color schemes for the UI. It uses JavaScript to interact with the browser's local storage and match media queries for theme adaptation.

```javascript
var palette=__md_get("__palette");if(palette&&palette.color){if("(prefers-color-scheme: light)"===palette.color.media){var media=matchMedia("(prefers-color-scheme: light)"),input=document.querySelector(media.matches?"[data-md-color-media='(prefers-color-scheme: light)']":"[data-md-color-media='(prefers-color-scheme: dark)']");palette.color.media=input.getAttribute("data-md-color-media"),palette.color.scheme=input.getAttribute("data-md-color-scheme"),palette.color.primary=input.getAttribute("data-md-color-primary"),palette.color.accent=input.getAttribute("data-md-color-accent")}for(var[key,value]of Object.entries(palette.color))document.body.setAttribute("data-md-color-"+key,value)}
```

### Database Operations with BungeeCord Player

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/site/plugin/index.html

Handles asynchronous database operations for player data in a BungeeCord environment. It uses `Dispatchers.IO` for database tasks and interacts with `ProxiedPlayer` for player information.

```kotlin
import kotlinx.coroutines.Dispatchers
import kotlinx.coroutines.withContext
import net.md_5.bungee.api.connection.ProxiedPlayer
import java.util.*

class Database() {
    suspend fun createDbIfNotExist() {
        println("[createDbIfNotExist] Start on any thread " + Thread.currentThread().name)
        withContext(Dispatchers.IO){
            println("[createDbIfNotExist] Creating database on database io thread " + Thread.currentThread().name)
            // ... create tables
        }
        println("[createDbIfNotExist] End on bungeecord plugin threadpool " + Thread.currentThread().name)
    }

    suspend fun getDataFromPlayer(player : ProxiedPlayer) : PlayerData {
        println("[getDataFromPlayer] Start on any thread " + Thread.currentThread().name)
        val playerData = withContext(Dispatchers.IO) {
            println("[getDataFromPlayer] Retrieving player data on database io thread " + Thread.currentThread().name)
            // ... get from database by player uuid or create new playerData instance.
            PlayerData(player.uniqueId, player.name, Date(), Date())
        }

        println("[getDataFromPlayer] End on bungeecord plugin threadpool " + Thread.currentThread().name)
        return playerData;
    }

    suspend fun saveData(player : ProxiedPlayer, playerData : PlayerData) {
        println("[saveData] Start on any thread " + Thread.currentThread().name)

        withContext(Dispatchers.IO){
            println("[saveData] Saving player data on database io thread " + Thread.currentThread().name)
            // insert or update playerData
        }

        println("[saveData] End on bungeecord plugin threadpool " + Thread.currentThread().name)
    }
}
```

### Initialize Dark Mode Preference

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/apidocs/mccoroutine-root/com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.minestom/-m-c-coroutine-exception-event/extension.html

This script initializes the dark mode preference for the documentation website based on local storage settings or operating system preferences. It applies a 'theme-dark' class to the HTML element if dark mode is enabled.

```javascript
var pathToRoot = "../../../";
document.documentElement.classList.replace("no-js","js");
const storage = localStorage.getItem("dokka-dark-mode");
if (storage == null) {
    const osDarkSchemePreferred = window.matchMedia && window.matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)').matches;
    if (osDarkSchemePreferred === true) {
        document.getElementsByTagName("html")[0].classList.add("theme-dark");
    }
} else {
    const savedDarkMode = JSON.parse(storage);
    if(savedDarkMode === true) {
        document.getElementsByTagName("html")[0].classList.add("theme-dark");
    }
}
```

### MCCoroutine Sponge API Documentation

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/apidocs/mccoroutine-root/com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.sponge/index.html

This section provides API documentation for the mccoroutine library's integration with the Sponge API. It details interfaces, classes, properties, and functions for managing coroutines within Sponge plugins.

```APIDOC
Package: com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.sponge

Interfaces:
  - CoroutineSession: Facade of a coroutine session of a single plugin.
  - MCCoroutine: Interface for MCCoroutine functionality.
  - SuspendingCommandExecutor: Interface containing the method directing how a certain command will be executed.

Enums:
  - EventExecutionType: The mode how suspendable events are dispatched manually.

Classes:
  - MCCoroutineExceptionEvent(plugin: Any, exception: Throwable): A sponge event called when an exception is raised in one of the coroutines managed by MCCoroutine. Cancelling this exception prevents logging and allows custom logging.
  - SuspendingPluginContainer: Allows a plugin instance to become a suspending listener, enabling suspend in startup methods.

Abstract Classes:
  - SuspendingCommandElement(pluginContainer: Any, text: Any)

Properties:
  - asyncDispatcher (Any): Gets the plugin async dispatcher.
  - minecraftDispatcher (Any): Gets the plugin minecraft dispatcher.
  - scope (Any): Gets the plugin coroutine scope.

Extension Properties:
  - ticks (Int): Converts the number to ticks for use with delay(..). Minecraft ticks 20 times per second (50ms interval), but delay() requires milliseconds. 1 tick does not equal 50ms standalone; it sums up to 50ms when used with delay.

Functions:
  - launch: (Details not provided in the input text)
```

### MCCoroutine API Documentation

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/apidocs/navigation.html

Provides comprehensive API documentation for the MCCoroutine library, including core classes, functions, and configurations. This section details how to use coroutines within a Minecraft environment.

```APIDOC
CoroutineSession:
  Manages the lifecycle of coroutines within a Minecraft context.

executesSuspend():
  Launches a coroutine that executes a suspend function.
  Parameters:
    block: The suspend function to execute.

launch():
  Launches a coroutine.
  Parameters:
    block: The coroutine block to execute.

MCCoroutine:
  The main entry point for MCCoroutine functionality.

Companion:
  Provides companion object methods for MCCoroutine.

MCCoroutineConfiguration:
  Configuration options for MCCoroutine.
  Properties:
    dispatcher: The CoroutineDispatcher to use.
    shutdownStrategy: The strategy for shutting down coroutines.

mcCoroutineConfiguration():
  Returns the global MCCoroutineConfiguration.

MCCoroutineExceptionEvent:
  Represents an event when a coroutine throws an exception.

Companion:
  Provides companion object methods for MCCoroutineExceptionEvent.

minecraftDispatcher():
  Returns the Minecraft-specific CoroutineDispatcher.

scope():
  Returns the CoroutineScope associated with MCCoroutine.

ShutdownStrategy:
  Defines strategies for shutting down coroutines.
  Enum Values:
    MANUAL: Coroutines are shut down manually.

SuspendingCommand:
  Represents a command that can be suspended.

ticks():
  Returns a Flow that emits on each Minecraft tick.
```

```javascript
var pathToRoot = "../../";
document.documentElement.classList.replace("no-js","js");
const storage = localStorage.getItem("dokka-dark-mode")
if (storage == null) {
    const osDarkSchemePreferred = window.matchMedia && window.matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)').matches
    if (osDarkSchemePreferred === true) {
        document.getElementsByTagName("html")[0].classList.add("theme-dark")
    }
} else {
    const savedDarkMode = JSON.parse(storage)
    if(savedDarkMode === true) {
        document.getElementsByTagName("html")[0].classList.add("theme-dark")
    }
}
```

```javascript
}
```

### Coroutine Timings for Launch Block

```kotlin
plugin.launch(plugin.minecraftDispatcher + object : CoroutineTimings() {}) {
    println("Please say hello in 2 seconds")
    delay(2000) // Delay for 2000 milliseconds
    println("hello")
}
```

### MCCoroutine Velocity API Documentation

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/apidocs/mccoroutine-root/com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.velocity/index.html

API documentation for MCCoroutine's Velocity integration, covering coroutine scope, dispatchers, launching coroutines, and registering suspending commands and listeners.

```APIDOC
Package: com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.velocity

Interfaces:
  CoroutineSession: Facade of a coroutine session of a single plugin.
  MCCoroutine: Main interface for MCCoroutine functionality.
  MCCoroutineExceptionEvent: Velocity event for coroutine exceptions. Cancelling it prevents logging.
  SuspendingPluginContainer: Manages coroutine sessions for plugins, allowing suspend functions in initialization.
  SuspendingSimpleCommand: Interface for suspending simple commands.
  SuspendInvocableCommand<I>: Interface for invocable suspending commands.

Properties:
  scope: Gets the plugin coroutine scope.
  velocityDispatcher: Gets the plugin's Velocity dispatcher for coroutines.

Functions:
  executesSuspend<S, T>(plugin: Any, command: suspend (S) -> Int): T
    Allows registering a suspending command. Takes the plugin instance and a suspend lambda as input.

  launch(context: CoroutineContext = velocityDispatcher, start: CoroutineStart = CoroutineStart.DEFAULT, block: suspend () -> Unit): Job
    Launches a new coroutine on the Velocity Plugin ThreadPool without blocking the current thread. Returns a Job reference.

  registerSuspend(plugin: Any, listener: Any)
    Registers an event listener with suspending functions, enabling suspension in event handlers.
```

### Bukkit/Other Dispatcher Usage

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/site/coroutine/index.html

```kotlin
import kotlinx.coroutines.Dispatchers
import kotlinx.coroutines.withContext
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.bukkit.launch // Example import, adjust for your platform

// Assuming 'plugin' is an instance of your plugin class with launch scope
// fun foo(plugin: YourPluginClass) {
//     plugin.launch {
//         // This will always be on the minecraft main thread.
//         val result1 = withContext(plugin.minecraftDispatcher) {
//             // Perform operations on the minecraft main thread.
//             "Player is " // Optionally, return a result.
//         }
//
//         // Prefer using Dispatchers.IO instead of asyncDispatcher
//         val result2 = withContext(Dispatchers.IO) {
//             // Perform operations asynchronously.
//             " Max"
//         }
//
//         println(result1 + result2) // Prints 'Player is Max'
//     }
// }
```

### Sponge Database Operations

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/site/plugin/index.html

Provides asynchronous database management for SpongeAPI-based Minecraft servers. This implementation uses Kotlin coroutines to ensure database interactions do not block the main server thread. The `Database` class facilitates database creation, retrieval of player data using Sponge's `Player` entity, and data persistence.

```kotlin
import kotlinx.coroutines.Dispatchers
import kotlinx.coroutines.withContext
import org.spongepowered.api.entity.living.player.Player
import java.util.*

class Database() {
    suspend fun createDbIfNotExist() {
        println("[createDbIfNotExist] Start on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
        withContext(Dispatchers.IO){
            println("[createDbIfNotExist] Creating database on database io thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
            // ... create tables
        }
        println("[createDbIfNotExist] End on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
    }

    suspend fun getDataFromPlayer(player : Player) : PlayerData {
        println("[getDataFromPlayer] Start on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
        val playerData = withContext(Dispatchers.IO) {
            println("[getDataFromPlayer] Retrieving player data on database io thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
            // ... get from database by player uuid or create new playerData instance.
            PlayerData(player.uniqueId, player.name, Date(), Date())
        }

        println("[getDataFromPlayer] End on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
        return playerData;
    }

    suspend fun saveData(player : Player, playerData : PlayerData) {
        println("[saveData] Start on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)

        withContext(Dispatchers.IO){
            println("[saveData] Saving player data on database io thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
            // insert or update playerData
        }

        println("[saveData] End on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
    }
}
```

--------------------------------

### Database Operations with Coroutines

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/site/plugin/index.html

Demonstrates asynchronous database operations using Kotlin coroutines. It shows how to switch to an IO dispatcher for database calls and return to the main thread.

```kotlin
import kotlinx.coroutines.Dispatchers
import kotlinx.coroutines.withContext
import org.bukkit.entity.Player
import java.util.*

class PlayerData(var uuid: UUID, var name: String, var lastJoinDate: Date, var lastQuitDate: Date) { }

class Database() {
    suspend fun createDbIfNotExist() {
        println("[createDbIfNotExist] Start on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
        withContext(Dispatchers.IO){
            println("[createDbIfNotExist] Creating database on database io thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
            // ... create tables
        }
        println("[createDbIfNotExist] End on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
    }

    suspend fun getDataFromPlayer(player : Player) : PlayerData {
        println("[getDataFromPlayer] Start on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
        val playerData = withContext(Dispatchers.IO) {
            println("[getDataFromPlayer] Retrieving player data on database io thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
            // ... get from database by player uuid or create new playerData instance.
            PlayerData(player.uniqueId, player.name, Date(), Date())
        }

        println("[getDataFromPlayer] End on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
        return playerData;
    }

    suspend fun saveData(player : Player, playerData : PlayerData) {
        println("[saveData] Start on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)

        withContext(Dispatchers.IO){
            println("[saveData] Saving player data on database io thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
            // insert or update playerData
        }

        println("[saveData] End on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
    }
}
```

### Integrating MCCoroutine with Fabric Server Lifecycle

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/site/listener/index.html

Demonstrates how to integrate MCCoroutine with the Fabric server lifecycle, specifically for Fabric mods. It shows how to set the Minecraft executor and launch coroutines for server startup and shutdown events, including registering custom event callbacks.

```kotlin
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.core.MCCoroutineConfiguration
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.core.launch
import net.fabricmc.api.DedicatedServerModInitializer
import net.minecraft.server.MinecraftServer
import net.minecraft.util.ActionResult
import net.minecraft.util.Hand
import net.minecraft.util.hit.HitResult
import java.util.concurrent.Executor

class MCCoroutineSampleServerMod : DedicatedServerModInitializer {
    override fun onInitializeServer() {
        ServerLifecycleEvents.SERVER_STARTING.register(ServerLifecycleEvents.ServerStarting {
            server ->
            mcCoroutineConfiguration.minecraftExecutor = Executor { r ->
                server.submitAndJoin(r)
            }
            launch {
                onServerStarting(server)
            }
        })

        ServerLifecycleEvents.SERVER_STOPPING.register {
            server ->
            mcCoroutineConfiguration.disposePluginSession()
        }
    }

    private suspend fun onServerStarting(server: MinecraftServer) {
        val database = Database()
        database.createDbIfNotExist()

        val listener = PlayerDataListener(database)
        val mod = this
        AttackEntityCallback.EVENT.register(AttackEntityCallback {
            player, world, hand, entity, hitResult ->
            mod.launch {
                listener.onPlayerAttackEvent(player, world, hand, entity, hitResult)
            }
            ActionResult.PASS
        })
    }
}
```

### MCCoroutineConfiguration API

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/apidocs/mccoroutine-root/com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.fabric/-m-c-coroutine-configuration/index.html

Defines the MCCoroutineConfiguration interface for customizing MCCoroutine behavior. It includes properties for logger, minecraftExecutor, and shutdownStrategy, as well as a function to dispose of plugin sessions.

```APIDOC
MCCoroutineConfiguration:
  interface
  Additional configurations for MCCoroutine and communication.

  Properties:
    logger: abstract var logger: Logger
      The logger being used by MCCoroutine.
    minecraftExecutor: abstract var minecraftExecutor: Executor
      The executor being used to schedule tasks on the main thread of minecraft. Can be retrieved from the MinecraftServer instance.
    shutdownStrategy: abstract var shutdownStrategy: ShutdownStrategy
      Strategy handling how MCCoroutine is disposed. Defaults to ShutdownStrategy.SCHEDULER.

  Functions:
    disposePluginSession: abstract fun disposePluginSession()
      Manually disposes the MCCoroutine session for the current extension or server.
```

### MCCoroutineSamplePlugin Main Class

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/docs/tasks.md

This is the main plugin class for a Bukkit server using McCrotoutine. It extends SuspendingJavaPlugin and demonstrates how to enable the plugin asynchronously, register suspending event listeners, and set suspending command executors.

```kotlin
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.bukkit.SuspendingJavaPlugin
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.bukkit.registerSuspendingEvents
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.bukkit.setSuspendingExecutor

class MCCoroutineSamplePlugin : SuspendingJavaPlugin() {
    private val database = Database() // Assuming Database class exists
    private val miniGame = MiniGame()

    override suspend fun onEnableAsync() {
        // Minecraft Main Thread
        database.createDbIfNotExist()
        server.pluginManager.registerSuspendingEvents(PlayerDataListener(database), this) // Assuming PlayerDataListener exists
        getCommand("playerdata")!!.setSuspendingExecutor(PlayerDataCommandExecutor(database)) // Assuming PlayerDataCommandExecutor exists
        server.pluginManager.registerSuspendingEvents(MiniGameListener(miniGame), this)
    }

    override suspend fun onDisableAsync() {
        // Minecraft Main Thread
    }
}
```

### Initialize Dark Mode

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/apidocs/mccoroutine-root/com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.bukkit/-shutdown-strategy/value-of.html

This script initializes the dark mode preference based on local storage or OS preference. It adds a 'theme-dark' class to the HTML element if dark mode is enabled.

```javascript
var pathToRoot = "../../../";
document.documentElement.classList.replace("no-js","js");
const storage = localStorage.getItem("dokka-dark-mode");
if (storage == null) {
    const osDarkSchemePreferred = window.matchMedia && window.matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)').matches;
    if (osDarkSchemePreferred === true) {
        document.getElementsByTagName("html")[0].classList.add("theme-dark");
    }
} else {
    const savedDarkMode = JSON.parse(storage);
    if(savedDarkMode === true) {
        document.getElementsByTagName("html")[0].classList.add("theme-dark");
    }
}
```

### Database Operations with Coroutines (Folia)

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/docs/plugin.md

Demonstrates asynchronous database operations using Kotlin coroutines within a Folia environment. It shows how to switch to the IO dispatcher for database tasks like creation, data retrieval, and saving, ensuring the main thread remains responsive.

```kotlin
import kotlinx.coroutines.Dispatchers
import kotlinx.coroutines.withContext
import org.bukkit.entity.Player
import java.util.*

class Database() {
    suspend fun createDbIfNotExist() {
        println("[createDbIfNotExist] Start on the caller thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
        withContext(Dispatchers.IO){
            println("[createDbIfNotExist] Creating database on database io thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
            // ... create tables
        }
        println("[createDbIfNotExist] End on the caller thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
    }

    suspend fun getDataFromPlayer(player : Player) : PlayerData {
        println("[getDataFromPlayer] Start on the caller thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
        val playerData = withContext(Dispatchers.IO) {
            println("[getDataFromPlayer] Retrieving player data on database io thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
            // ... get from database by player uuid or create new playerData instance.
            PlayerData(player.uniqueId, player.name, Date(), Date())
        }

        println("[getDataFromPlayer] End on the caller thread  " + Thread.currentThread().id)
        return playerData;
    }
  
    suspend fun saveData(player : Player, playerData : PlayerData) {
        println("[saveData] Start on the caller thread  " + Thread.currentThread().id)

        withContext(Dispatchers.IO){
            println("[saveData] Saving player data on database io thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
            // insert or update playerData
        }

        println("[saveData] End on the caller thread  " + Thread.currentThread().id)
    }
}
```

### MCCoroutine Core JavaScript Functions

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/site/404.html

This snippet defines core JavaScript functions for MCCoroutine, including theme management (`__md_get`, `__md_set`) and scope handling. These functions are essential for the dynamic behavior and theming of the documentation site.

```javascript
MCCoroutine :root {
  --md-text-font: "Roboto";
  --md-code-font: "Roboto Mono";
}
__md_scope = new URL("/", location),
__md_hash = e => [...e].reduce(((e, _) => (e << 5) - e + _.charCodeAt(0)), 0),
__md_get = (e, _ = localStorage, t = __md_scope) => JSON.parse(_.getItem(t.pathname + "." + e)),
__md_set = (e, _, t = localStorage, a = __md_scope) => {
  try {
    t.setItem(a.pathname + "." + e, JSON.stringify(_));
  } catch (e) {}
}
```

### Bukkit Sync Task Extension (Kotlin)

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/ARTICLE.md

A Kotlin extension function for the Any class to easily run tasks synchronously on the Bukkit server scheduler. It supports optional delay and repeating intervals.

```kotlin
/**
 * Executes the given [f] via the [plugin] synchronized with the server tick.
 */
fun Any.sync(plugin : Plugin, delayTicks: Long = 0L, repeatingTicks: Long = 0L, f: () -> Unit) {
    if (repeatingTicks > 0) {
        plugin.server.scheduler.runTaskTimer(plugin, f, delayTicks, repeatingTicks)
    } else {
        plugin.server.scheduler.runTaskLater(plugin, f, delayTicks)
    }
}
```

Provides access to core MCCoroutine functionalities within a Minestom environment. This includes managing coroutine sessions, configurations, and dispatchers for both extensions and the server.

```APIDOC
interface CoroutineSession
  // Facade of a coroutine session of a single extension or entire server.

interface MCCoroutine
  // Hidden internal MCCoroutine interface.

interface MCCoroutineConfiguration
  // Additional configurations for MCCoroutine and communication.

class MCCoroutineExceptionEvent(val extension: Any?, val exception: Throwable)
  // A Minestom event which is called when an exception is raised in one of the coroutines managed by MCCoroutine. Cancelling this exception causes the error to not get logged and offers to possibility for custom logging.

enum class ShutdownStrategy : Enum<ShutdownStrategy>
  // See https://shynixn.github.io/MCCoroutine/wiki/site/plugindisable for more details.

val Any.asyncDispatcher: CoroutineContext
  // Gets the extension async dispatcher.

val Any.asyncDispatcher: CoroutineContext
  // Gets the server async dispatcher.

val Any.mcCoroutineConfiguration: MCCoroutineConfiguration
  // Gets the configuration instance of MCCoroutine.

  // Gets the configuration instance of MCCoroutine.

val Any.minecraftDispatcher: CoroutineContext
  // Gets the extension minecraft dispatcher.

val Any.minecraftDispatcher: CoroutineContext
  // Gets the server minecraft dispatcher.

val Any.scope: Any
  // Gets the extension coroutine scope.

val Any.scope: Any
  // Gets the extension coroutine scope.

val Int.ticks: Long
  // Converts the number to ticks for being used together with delay(..). E.g. delay(1.ticks). Minecraft ticks 20 times per second, which means a tick appears every 50 milliseconds. However, delay() does not directly work with the MineStomScheduler and needs millisecond manipulation to work as expected. Therefore, 1 tick does not equal 50 milliseconds when using this method standalone and only sums up to 50 milliseconds if you use it together with delay.
```

### Database Operations with Coroutines (Minestom)

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/docs/plugin.md

Illustrates asynchronous database operations using Kotlin coroutines within a Minestom environment. It showcases the use of `withContext(Dispatchers.IO)` for executing database-related tasks on a separate thread, ensuring non-blocking operations.

```kotlin
import kotlinx.coroutines.Dispatchers
import kotlinx.coroutines.withContext
import net.minestom.server.entity.Player
import java.util.*

class Database() {
    suspend fun createDbIfNotExist() {
        println("[createDbIfNotExist] Start on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
        withContext(Dispatchers.IO){
            println("[createDbIfNotExist] Creating database on database io thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
            // ... create tables
        }
        println("[createDbIfNotExist] End on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
    }

    suspend fun getDataFromPlayer(player : Player) : PlayerData {
        println("[getDataFromPlayer] Start on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
        val playerData = withContext(Dispatchers.IO) {
            println("[getDataFromPlayer] Retrieving player data on database io thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
            // ... get from database by player uuid or create new playerData instance.
            PlayerData(player.uuid, player.username, Date(), Date())
        }

        println("[getDataFromPlayer] End on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
        return playerData;
    }

    suspend fun saveData(player : Player, playerData : PlayerData) {
        println("[saveData] Start on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)

        withContext(Dispatchers.IO){
            println("[saveData] Saving player data on database io thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
            // insert or update playerData
        }

        println("[saveData] End on minecraft thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
    }
}
```

### Fabric Server Initialization and Event Handling

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/docs/listener.md

Illustrates how to initialize MCCoroutine for a Fabric server mod and register event listeners for server lifecycle events and player actions. It demonstrates setting the Minecraft executor and launching coroutines for event handling.

```kotlin
import net.fabricmc.api.DedicatedServerModInitializer
import net.minecraft.server.MinecraftServer
import net.fabricmc.fabric.api.event.lifecycle.v1.ServerLifecycleEvents
import net.fabricmc.fabric.api.event.player.AttackEntityCallback
import net.minecraft.util.ActionResult
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.fabric.minecraftExecutor
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.fabric.mcCoroutineConfiguration
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.fabric.launch
import java.util.concurrent.Executor
import kotlin.coroutines.CoroutineContext

class MCCoroutineSampleServerMod : DedicatedServerModInitializer {
    override fun onInitializeServer() {
        ServerLifecycleEvents.SERVER_STARTING.register(ServerLifecycleEvents.ServerStarting { server ->
            // Connect Native Minecraft Scheduler and MCCoroutine.
            mcCoroutineConfiguration.minecraftExecutor = Executor { r ->
                server.submitAndJoin(r)
            }
            launch {
                onServerStarting(server)
            }
        })

        ServerLifecycleEvents.SERVER_STOPPING.register { server ->
            mcCoroutineConfiguration.disposePluginSession()
        }
    }

    /**
     * MCCoroutine is ready after the server has started.
     */
    private suspend fun onServerStarting(server : MinecraftServer) {
        // Minecraft Main Thread
        val database = Database()
        database.createDbIfNotExist()

        val listener = PlayerDataListener(database)
        val mod = this
        AttackEntityCallback.EVENT.register(AttackEntityCallback { player, world, hand, entity, hitResult ->
            mod.launch {
                listener.onPlayerAttackEvent(player, world, hand, entity, hitResult)
            }
            ActionResult.PASS
        })
    }
}
```

--------------------------------

### Parallel Async Operations with Coroutines

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/ARTICLE.md

Illustrates advanced coroutine usage with `async` for parallel execution of multiple asynchronous tasks. It shows how to `await` results from these tasks and combine them, highlighting the performance benefit of parallel processing.

```kotlin
@EventHandler
fun onPlayerInteractEvent(event: PlayerInteractEvent) {
    println("Event on Thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
    
    // Launch always starts a coroutine.
    GlobalScope.launch(Dispatchers.minecraft) {
        println("Launch-1 on Thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)

        // Use withContext for dispatching async operations.
        val data = withContext(Dispatchers.async) {
            Thread.sleep(1000)
            println("Async-1-Database on Thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
            arrayListOf("give-apple")
        }

        // Once the data is here, continue on the main thread.
        println("Launch-2 on Thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)

        if (data.contains("give-apple")) {
            println("Launch-3 on Thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
            val currentMilliSeconds = System.currentTimeMillis()

            // Use async for parallel async operations.
            val appleConfigTask = async(Dispatchers.async) {
                Thread.sleep(2000)
                println("Async-2-Config on Thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
                arrayListOf("life-time")
            }

            // Use async for parallel async operations.
            val appleDefinitionTask = async(Dispatchers.async) {
                Thread.sleep(3000)
                println("Async-3-Definition on Thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)
                arrayListOf("definition")
            }

            println("Launch-4 on Thread " + Thread.currentThread().id)

            // Wait until both tasks are finished. 
            // This does not block the main thread either.
            val appleConfig = appleConfigTask.await()
            val appleDefinition = appleDefinitionTask.await()

            // As both tasks get executed parallel, it only took ~3 seconds instead of ~5 seconds to finish them.
            val milliSeconds = System.currentTimeMillis() - currentMilliSeconds
            println("Launch-5 on Thread " + Thread.currentThread().id + " after " + milliSeconds + ".")
            
            if (appleConfig.contains("life-time") && appleDefinition.contains("definition")) {
                println("Good job! You have understood coroutines!")
            }
        }
    }
 }
 
 // Execution Order: Event, Launch-1, Async-1, Launch-2
```

### Initialize Dark Mode and Theme

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/apidocs/mccoroutine-root/com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.fabric/ticks.html

This script initializes dark mode based on local storage settings or OS preference. It also handles toggling the theme.

```javascript
var pathToRoot = "../../";
document.documentElement.classList.replace("no-js","js");
const storage = localStorage.getItem("dokka-dark-mode");
if (storage == null) {
    const osDarkSchemePreferred = window.matchMedia && window.matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)').matches;
    if (osDarkSchemePreferred === true) {
        document.getElementsByTagName("html")[0].classList.add("theme-dark");
    }
} else {
    const savedDarkMode = JSON.parse(storage);
    if(savedDarkMode === true) {
        document.getElementsByTagName("html")[0].classList.add("theme-dark");
    }
}
```

### Sponge Plugin: Registering Suspending Listeners and Commands

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/docs/commandexecutor.md

This Kotlin code demonstrates how to set up a Sponge plugin using MCCoroutine. It shows the registration of a suspending event listener and a suspending command executor, along with basic database interaction.

```kotlin
import org.spongepowered.api.command.spec.CommandSpec
import org.spongepowered.api.event.Listener
import org.spongepowered.api.event.game.state.GameStartedServerEvent
import org.spongepowered.api.event.game.state.GameStoppingServerEvent
import org.spongepowered.api.plugin.Plugin
import org.spongepowered.api.plugin.PluginContainer
import org.spongepowered.api.text.Text
import com.google.inject.Inject
import org.spongepowered.api.Sponge
import org.spongepowered.api.command.args.GenericArguments

@Plugin(
    id = "mccoroutine sample",
    name = "MCCoroutineSample",
    description = "MCCoroutineSample is sample plugin to use MCCoroutine in Sponge."
)
class MCCoroutineSamplePlugin {
    private val database = Database()

    @Inject
    private lateinit var suspendingPluginContainer: SuspendingPluginContainer

    @Inject
    private lateinit var pluginContainer: PluginContainer

    @Listener
    suspend fun onEnable(event: GameStartedServerEvent) {
        // Minecraft Main Thread
        database.createDbIfNotExist()
        Sponge.getEventManager().registerSuspendingListeners(pluginContainer, PlayerDataListener(database))
        val commandSpec = CommandSpec.builder()
            .description(Text.of("Command for operations."))
            .permission("mccoroutine.sample")
            .arguments(
                GenericArguments.onlyOne(GenericArguments.string(Text.of("name")))
            )
            .suspendingExecutor(pluginContainer, PlayerDataCommandExecutor(database))
        Sponge.getCommandManager().register(pluginContainer, commandSpec.build(), listOf("playerdata"))
    }

    @Listener
    suspend fun onDisable(event: GameStoppingServerEvent) {
        // Minecraft Main Thread
    }
}
```

### Syntactic Sugar for Dispatchers

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/ARTICLE.md

Extends the Kotlin Coroutines `Dispatchers` object to provide easy access to the custom Minecraft and asynchronous dispatchers via `Dispatchers.minecraft` and `Dispatchers.async` respectively.

```kotlin
/**
 * Minecraft async dispatcher.
 */
val Dispatchers.async: CoroutineContext
    get() =  DispatcherContainer.async

/**
 * Minecraft sync dispatcher.
 */
val Dispatchers.minecraft: CoroutineContext
    get() =  DispatcherContainer.sync
```

### BungeeCord Dispatcher with UNDISPATCHED

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/site/coroutine/index.html

Demonstrates how to use `CoroutineStart.UNDISPATCHED` with `plugin.launch` on BungeeCord to execute the coroutine in the current tick, even when switching to a different dispatcher like `Dispatchers.IO`.

```kotlin
fun foo() {
    plugin.launch(start = CoroutineStart.UNDISPATCHED) {
        // This is the same thread before calling plugin.launch

        val result = withContext(Dispatchers.IO) {
            // Perform operations asynchronously.
            "Playxer is Max"
        }

        // Here we are automatically back on a new random thread on the bungeeCord threadPool.
        println(result) // Prints 'Player is Max'
    }
}
```

--------------------------------

### Bukkit Async Task Extension (Kotlin)

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/ARTICLE.md

A Kotlin extension function for the Any class to easily run tasks asynchronously on the Bukkit server scheduler. It supports optional delay and repeating intervals.

```kotlin
/**
 * Executes the given [f] via the [plugin] asynchronous.
 */
fun Any.async(plugin : Plugin, delayTicks: Long = 0L, repeatingTicks: Long = 0L, f: () -> Unit) {
    if (repeatingTicks > 0) {
        plugin.server.scheduler.runTaskTimerAsynchronously(plugin, f, delayTicks, repeatingTicks)
    } else {
        plugin.server.scheduler.runTaskLaterAsynchronously(plugin, f, delayTicks)
    }
}
```

### Bukkit Asynchronous Task (Java)

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/ARTICLE.md

Demonstrates scheduling an asynchronous task in Bukkit using Java's server scheduler. It involves fetching data and then scheduling a synchronous task to update the player's inventory.

```java
private Plugin plugin; // Initialized plugin.

@EventHandler
public void onPlayerInteractEvent(PlayerInteractEvent event) {
    plugin.getServer().getScheduler().runTaskAsynchronously(plugin, () -> {
        List<String> data = getDataFromDatabase();
        
        plugin.getServer().getScheduler().runTask(plugin, () -> {
                
            if(data.contains("give-apple")) {
                event.getPlayer().getInventory().addItem(new ItemStack(Material.APPLE))
            }
        });
    });
}
```

### Launch Coroutine

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/apidocs/mccoroutine-root/com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.bungeecord/launch.html

Launches a new coroutine on the BungeeCord Plugin ThreadPool without blocking the current thread. The coroutine is cancelled when the resulting job is Job.cancel. Uncaught exceptions are logged to Plugin.getLogger.

```APIDOC
launch
======

fun <Error class: unknown class>.[launch](launch.html)(context: [CoroutineContext](https://kotlinlang.org/api/latest/jvm/stdlib/kotlin-stdlib/kotlin.coroutines/-coroutine-context/index.html) = bungeeCordDispatcher, start: <Error class: unknown class> = CoroutineStart.DEFAULT, block: suspend <Error class: unknown class>.() -> [Unit](https://kotlinlang.org/api/latest/jvm/stdlib/kotlin-stdlib/kotlin/-unit/index.html)): <Error class: unknown class>

Launches a new coroutine on the BungeeCord Plugin ThreadPool without blocking the current thread and returns a reference to the coroutine as a Job. The coroutine is cancelled when the resulting job is Job.cancel.

The coroutine context is inherited from a Plugin.scope. Additional context elements can be specified with [context](launch.html) argument. If the context does not have any dispatcher nor any other [ContinuationInterceptor](https://kotlinlang.org/api/latest/jvm/stdlib/kotlin-stdlib/kotlin.coroutines/-continuation-interceptor/index.html), then Plugin.bungeeCordDispatcher is used. The parent job is inherited from a Plugin.scope as well, but it can also be overridden with a corresponding [context](launch.html) element.

By default, the coroutine is scheduled on the BungeeCord thread pool which is executed later. Other start options can be specified via `start` parameter. See CoroutineStart for details. An optional [start](launch.html) parameter can be set to CoroutineStart.LAZY to start coroutine _lazily_. In this case, the coroutine Job is created in _new_ state. It can be explicitly started with Job.start function and will be started implicitly on the first invocation of Job.join.

Uncaught exceptions in this coroutine do not cancel the parent job or any other child jobs. All uncaught exceptions are logged to Plugin.getLogger by default.

#### Parameters

context
  The coroutine context to start. Should almost be always be Plugin.bungeeCordDispatcher. Async operations should be be created using withContext after using the default parameters of this method.

start
  coroutine start option. The default value is CoroutineStart.DEFAULT.

block
  the coroutine code which will be invoked in the context of the provided scope.
```

### MCCoroutineSamplePlugin Integration

Source: https://github.com/shynixn/mccoroutine/blob/master/docs/wiki/site/tasks/index.html

The main `SuspendingJavaPlugin` class that sets up the plugin. It demonstrates how to register suspending event listeners (`MiniGameListener`, `PlayerDataListener`) and suspending command executors (`PlayerDataCommandExecutor`) using the `mccoroutine` library's utilities.

```kotlin
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.bukkit.SuspendingJavaPlugin
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.bukkit.registerSuspendingEvents
import com.github.shynixn.mccoroutine.bukkit.setSuspendingExecutor

class MCCoroutineSamplePlugin : SuspendingJavaPlugin() {
    private val database = Database()
    private val miniGame = MiniGame()

    override suspend fun onEnableAsync() {
        database.createDbIfNotExist()
        server.pluginManager.registerSuspendingEvents(PlayerDataListener(database), this)
        getCommand("playerdata")!!.setSuspendingExecutor(PlayerDataCommandExecutor(database))
        server.pluginManager.registerSuspendingEvents(MiniGameListener(miniGame), this)
    }

    override suspend fun onDisableAsync() {
        // Minecraft Main Thread
    }
}
```
